The count-destruction probe compares intact, altered-count and names-only summaries on the pooled hard tier and on FR-Bank alone (Tables~\ref{tab:destruction} and~\ref{tab:destruction-fr}).
The pooled names-only row is driven by the structureless stores.
The FR-Bank row supports only the narrower conclusion that counts are not necessary for coverage, because every manipulated-condition interval on FR-Bank includes zero (Table~\ref{tab:destruction-fr}, with the differences and intervals read from the recorded probe scores).

\subsection{Factorial attribution}\label{app:factorial}

The factorial separates the lifecycle-index change from the two prompt changes and checks the main-study reproduction.
The lifecycle-index main effect is $+0.1980$, with intervals $[+0.1522,+0.2423]$ at item level and $[+0.1297,+0.2639]$ grouped by persona; the two prompt-fix effects are $+0.0134$ ($[-0.0105,+0.0354]$; $[-0.0073,+0.0348]$) and $-0.0193$ ($[-0.0451,+0.0034]$; $[-0.0396,+0.0011]$), both including zero.
The matching factorial condition reproduces the main study at $0.7344$ against its indexed NOK of $0.7339$; the intervals are recomputed from the recorded rows of the factorial, every cell rescored on the same bootstrap draw.

%% CHANGED (R92-1): the attribution paragraph relocated from Section 4.4 (CL-C060-C062); the section pointer
%% it carried is dropped here, where it would point at itself.
After the pilot's null obligation result the index gained its lifecycle columns and the prompt gained worked examples and a detection step, so the NOK redesign changed three things at once.
The attribution comparison estimates the effect of each of the three changes.
The lifecycle-index effect dominates both prompt effects, so the recovery is attributed to the lifecycle information, within the conditions we varied.

%%V11:1575-1766%%

\paragraph{What the summary does to the score.}
The recorded coverage scores on the two structureless stores show what the inversion consists of (Table~\ref{tab:dist}).
Blind, GPT-4.1 mini's score takes six distinct values with a variance of $0.0245$ on Mem0 and $0.0242$ on A-MEM.
Indexed under the constrained rendering it takes five values with a variance of $0.0060$ and $0.0052$, and the mean falls from $0.22$ to $0.17$ on Mem0 and from $0.22$ to $0.16$ on A-MEM.
The summary lowers the score on $86$ Mem0 items and raises it on $46$, and lowers it on $110$ A-MEM items and raises it on $48$, while the mean on positives and on negatives moves together.
Compression alone cannot lower an AUROC below $0.50$, because AUROC is unchanged by any order-preserving transform; what the rows show is a collapse onto five values in which the positives average slightly below the negatives ($0.1617$ against $0.1688$ on Mem0, $0.1544$ against $0.1658$ on A-MEM), and that ordering is the inversion.
The store-written summaries, which carry no not-expressible line, show no loss (within tolerance of blind, above), and the name-bearing summary rebuilt from ground truth restores coverage (Table~\ref{tab:s1-names}), so the loss belongs to the constrained rendering; which line of it produces the collapse is the question the ablations above leave open, and the marker's wording is not it (Table~\ref{tab:marker}).

\subsection{Non-LLM baseline suite}\label{app:baselines}

The non-model readers of the summary carry numbered names in the registrations, and the main text calls the sixth the name lookup.
The registrations number them NB-1 to NB-6.
This appendix carries the whole family, the per-state split of the name lookup against the models, and the lookup on the coverage tiers, because each bounds a claim the main text makes about what a model adds.
Both readings were registered, the whole-population comparison as a sentence pair and the per-state one as a single reading.

Six non-model readers were run on the same serialized summaries the monitor reads, to ask whether a language model is needed at all.
The first is a deterministic lifecycle rule that parses per-category entry and lifecycle counts and assigns the question to a category by content-token overlap with that category's attribute names.
It scores coverage as the assigned category's share of the store's largest category and obligation as its superseded-plus-retracted-plus-expired share.
The second is a logistic model over numeric summary features only, label-supervised and persona-grouped.
The fourth and fifth are unsupervised query-to-summary lookups, lexical and embedding respectively, scored against the whole per-category name map.
The sixth is that same pair of lookups aimed at one section instead, the flat list under \texttt{ATTRIBUTE NAMES CARRYING SUPERSEDED CONTENT}, whose populations, tolerance and outcome sentences were fixed before it was scored.
The third was disqualified before scoring, because its features concatenated the summary with the question and its ablation showed question-only text outscoring the concatenated version on every population.
It read question templates rather than the store (supplement Section~S.1, item 12), and its column below is the summary-only ablation, which is the part that reads the store.

%%V11:1780-1806%%

Three readings carry beyond the table, because each bears on a claim the main text makes.
The first is the store-written obligation row, where the monitor at $0.5959$ is indistinguishable from the embedding lookup at $0.5974$, a difference of $-0.0015$ inside the tolerance: under corrupted self-description the monitor's obligation judgment falls back to what a similarity lookup already achieves, which is the same degradation the $42.3\%$ retention records in Section~\ref{sec:backends}, reached by an independent route.
The second is that the monitor's coverage margin over the whole-map readers, the lookups that score the question against the whole name map, is not uniform across backends.
It exists where lifecycle structure exists and vanishes where it does not, which is the same boundary the inversion result draws.
The third is the structureless pair, where the lexical and embedding lookups sit at $0.5000$ and the other readers scatter around chance ($0.4184$ to $0.5226$).
The monitor's indexed point estimates under the constrained rendering fall below them ($0.4777$, $0.4526$; Section~\ref{sec:backends}).

% AUX-E1R-NB6 (R81). U9 FIREWALL: this paragraph reports what NB-6 measures. It does NOT
% join the BELIEVED retention figure to the flat-lookup bound, and no connective here may
% be read as doing so. The 42.3% figure is deliberately absent from this paragraph.
The name lookup was registered as a direct test of one exposure: whether the obligation judgment under a ground-truth summary is close to reading the answer off the superseded-names section.
The whole-map lookups cannot detect that, because they dilute the section across the whole name map.
Under the ground-truth summary it reaches $0.7484$ against the monitor's $0.7339$, so on this population the monitor holds no margin over that reader.
The difference is inside the $\pm0.05$ tolerance, so it does not establish the lookup as the better judge either; what it removes is the claim of a margin.
Under the store-written summary it reaches $0.5858$ against the monitor's $0.5959$, also inside the tolerance.
The whole-population difference under the ground-truth summary, monitor minus lookup, is $-0.0145$ with item-level interval $[-0.0769,+0.0549]$ and persona-clustered interval $[-0.1080,+0.0765]$ (Table~\ref{tab:nb6-composition}, last row), so the interval and the tolerance reading agree.
The ground-truth superseded list averages $1.75$ names per item and the store-written list averages $15.04$, so the two summaries give the lookup very different amounts of text to work with.
Mem0 and A-MEM were not scored by the lookup, because their superseded-names section carries only the marker the renderer writes where a store exposes no field, and the lookup would measure the marker rather than the store.
%% ADDENDUM_R81 reproduction check [APPX] F.8, one sentence; frozen wording ships verbatim.
On the coverage rows NB-6 scores the whole per-category name map and reproduces NB-4 and NB-5 to four decimals, so the coverage cells print a dash rather than repeating them.
The lookup has one descriptive value on the cross-model set, whole-set and per-state values on the store-counterfactual set, and none on the authorship-matched set. The comparison between model and lookup is therefore complete on the full obligation population ($661$) and on the store-counterfactual set ($444$).
%% R131 close, W7 (2026-09-20): the body's sentence moves here as a pointer (Appendix C.2 prints the intervals, tab:cf-fok).
On the counterfactual set every model's coverage interval excludes chance (Table~\ref{tab:cf-fok}, Appendix~\ref{app:counterfactual}).
Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup} below adds the superseded section scored on the coverage rows, a measurement added after the main study.

The registered texts for these rows (Appendix~\ref{app:registration}) read as follows.
%% NB6-1 FAILS [MAIN] (registrations/ADDENDUM_R81.md section 6; AUX-E1R-NB6): frozen wording ships verbatim; moved here from Section 8 under
%% registrations/AMENDMENT_MAIN_PLACEMENT.md (R105, 2026-09-14; pending the author's ratification).
A matcher that scores each question directly against the store's list of names marked superseded reaches $0.7484$ on the same $661$ questions, against the monitor's $0.7339$.
Under a truthful summary the obligation judgment is available to a direct lookup on that one list.
%% AUX-E1R-NB6-PERSTATE, frozen reading (i) [MAIN] (ADDENDUM_R81 section 10.3 @ e8ba22c): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 8 (same amendment).
The superseded-names matcher matches the smallest model tested. Larger models score higher on the states the index only counts, an advantage the paired blind comparison does not attribute to the index (Tables~\ref{tab:nb6-attrib} and~\ref{tab:nb6-frontier}).
%% NB6-2 HOLDS [MAIN] (ADDENDUM_R81 Amendment 1; AUX-E1R-NB6): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 (same amendment). U9 FIREWALL: no connective
%% joins these two sentences to any retention sentence; the 42.3 percent figure is deliberately absent from this paragraph.
Under the metadata the backend actually exposes, the monitor's obligation judgment is no better than a matcher that reads the store's list of superseded names directly.
The monitor scores $0.5959$ on that row and the matcher $0.5858$ (Appendix~\ref{app:baselines}).

A census run after that result asks why the lookup works where it does, because the answer says what a reader can take from this summary and what it cannot.
The summary names attributes in exactly one place, the superseded-names section, and expiry and retraction reach the summary as counts and are never named.
Splitting the obligation items by the lifecycle state each obligation arises from reproduces that asymmetry exactly (Table~\ref{tab:nb6-composition}).
The lookup reaches $0.9534$ on supersession-derived items and sits at $0.4823$ on expiry and $0.4053$ on retraction, the last two at or below chance.
The monitor is weaker off supersession as well, at $0.5110$ and $0.5638$, and on retraction it is the higher of the two readers.
A check of whether each obligation's own attribute name appears in the section the lookup reads finds it for $74$ of $104$ under exact slot match: $63$ of $63$ supersession-derived, $11$ of $26$ retraction-derived, and none of the $15$ expiry-derived.
On the state the summary names, the lookup has the item's own name to read; whether the monitor reads it is a separate question that this census does not settle.
Where the self-description names an obligation, a lookup reaches it; where the self-description only counts it, the lookup does not.
Composition follows the same line: obligations on the full population are $63$ of $104$ supersession-derived, against $19$ of $40$ on the cross-model set, where the lookup scores $0.6023$.

%%V11:1844-1862%%

%% ADDENDUM_R81 sections 10.2 and 11.4: registered branch texts, frozen wording ships verbatim.
The monitor exceeds the matcher on retraction-derived obligations at $n=26$, by $+0.1585$ $[+0.0002,+0.3341]$.
That interval's lower bound sits at $+0.0002$, so the row is marginal and is read as marginal.
The index counts expired content, and neither reader recovers it.
Under persona clustering the mini per-state differences read Supersession $-0.0962$ item $[-0.1520,-0.0495]$ clustered $[-0.1712,-0.0307]$; Expiry $+0.0287$ item $[-0.1795,+0.2212]$ clustered $[-0.3115,+0.4296]$; Retraction $+0.1585$ item $[+0.0002,+0.3341]$ clustered $[-0.0560,+0.4152]$.
The retraction row is marginal at item level (lower bound $+0.0002$) and not separated under persona clustering.
The same split run on the three models scored on this population under five calls per question separates the two readers in opposite directions.
On supersession, the content the summary names, the lookup is above all three.
On expiry and retraction, the content the summary only counts, all three are above the lookup, and every difference in Table~\ref{tab:nb6-frontier} excludes zero.
Expiry rests on $15$ obligations and retraction on $26$, so what these rows support is the direction of the crossover rather than a calibrated size for it.
The comparison sets the models against an intent-blind reader, so it cannot say whether their advantage on the counted states comes from the store or from the question.
Answering that needs the paired gain on the same rows, which Table~\ref{tab:nb6-attrib} reports per state.
On supersession the summary carries every model, each difference excluding zero.
On expiry one model of three separates from its own blind condition and on retraction one does, so the models' advantage over the lookup on the counted states is not attributable to the summary.
GPT-4.1 mini is absent from that table because its blind condition spans two distinct values on this population.

\paragraph{The superseded-section lookup on the coverage tiers.}
The coverage rows of Table~\ref{tab:baselines} score the whole name map.
Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup} scores the superseded section alone on the same coverage populations, beside GPT-4.1 mini's recorded indexed coverage score.
On the hard tier every coverage positive is an obligation, so the embedding lookup on the superseded section reads $0.7864$ against the model's $0.7003$, and the difference of $-0.0861$ has intervals that include zero.
The FR-Bank easy tier has ordinary present records as its positives, and there the model reads $0.7835$ against the superseded-section lookup's $0.6083$, a reliable margin of $+0.1752$.
%% R112 (2026-09-16), post hoc F1 (reviews/NUMBERS_WP.md section 10, AUX-WP-F1-WHOLEMAP; reviews/v19/results/posthoc_f1.json).
A lookup over the whole name map, the reader the coverage rows of Table~\ref{tab:baselines} score, was computed on the easy tier after the fact and specified before recomputation: it reads $0.7469$ (persona interval $[0.6866,0.8028]$), and the model's margin over it is $+0.0367$ with intervals $[-0.0127,+0.0861]$ and $[-0.0036,+0.0776]$, not reliable and within tolerance, so the coverage margin the superseded-section lookup showed does not survive the stronger reader with no generative-model call (Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup}, last row).
Pooling the easy tier across backends drops the lookup to $0.4021$, because the structureless stores' sections carry only the marker, and the model's margin there is $+0.2315$.

\paragraph{The coverage judgment against its floor.}
%% R118 ruling 4 (2026-09-19): Section 8's coverage paragraph moved whole from the body; its pointer to this appendix now names the table. No number changes.
The coverage margin over the name lookup does not survive a lookup over the whole name map.
On the easy tier ($354$ questions), where the positives are ordinary present records, GPT-4.1 mini scores $0.78$ against the name lookup's $0.61$, a reliable margin.
%% R112 (2026-09-16), post hoc F1 (reviews/NUMBERS_WP.md section 10, AUX-WP-F1-WHOLEMAP): whole-map lookup 0.7469; model minus whole-map +0.0367 [-0.0127, +0.0861] / [-0.0036, +0.0776].
A lookup over every name in the map, computed post hoc, scores $0.75$ on the same questions, and the model's margin over it is $+0.04$ with interval $[-0.01,+0.09]$, which is not a reliable one (Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup}).
On the hard tier every coverage positive is an obligation, so the coverage and obligation scores are one row, and the lookup's $0.79$ against the model's $0.70$ is not a reliable difference.
FOK detects a missing topic and not a single deleted record, which is NOK's target (Appendix~\ref{app:construction}).
The score ranks well and calibrates badly (Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc}).

\subsection{Census of the store-written state}\label{app:census}

Table~\ref{tab:census} in Section~\ref{sec:backends} carries the census behind the over-mark, overwrite and unresolved-links reading.
%% R123 Phase D (2026-09-19): the four counts near one hundred reconciled in one sentence (AUX-BELIEVED-FR: 109 events, approximately 102 explained; AUX-SIDE-S2-CENSUS: 104 TRUE-superseded entries, 40 active; the 104 obligations: 63 / 26 / 15).
Four counts near one hundred in that census are four different things: the manifests record $109$ supersession events across the forty personas; the ground-truth join explains about $102$ of the store's inactive entries; the manifest's supersessions touch $104$ stored entries, $40$ of them still active; and the $104$ obligations of the full obligation set are questions, $63$ supersession, $26$ retraction and $15$ expiry, not entries; the $72\%$ of Table~\ref{tab:census} is a share of the $109$ events ($78$ of them), not of the $104$ entries.
This appendix records the store-written superseded list, the renderer's cap, and the fourth-backend trial, because each bears on how much of the loss is the store's.

%% Writing pass Phase 1 (2026-09-15), items 52 and 90: the 5% provenance sentence from Section 4 and the body's Conflate row (AUX-SIDE-S2-CENSUS; the R109 write-path census) as prose.
\paragraph{Two census facts the body states in brief.}
The pass rate of $5\%$ that Section~\ref{sec:setup} cites for FR-Bank's mask is the released store's own figure, measured on its $40$ retraction questions with the mask in place \citep{fr}.
The store also conflates retraction with supersession: of $31$ entries marked retracted across the $40$ personas, $22$ share a slot key with another entry and $16$ carry a supersedes pointer, so the write path records retraction through the supersession mechanism and the backend's own metadata cannot separate retracted from superseded content.
The manifests record $40$ retraction events, one per persona, and the ground-truth join resolves $9$ of them to a retracted entry ($23$ entries).
%% R126 addendum A2 (2026-09-20): Table 2's caption notes moved here (the matching threshold; the two reproduction scripts; the conflation note is the sentence above).
The count of retained active assertions in Table~\ref{tab:census} depends on the stored-versus-obsolete matching threshold of the census, and the artifact's \texttt{repro\_mem0\_erase.py} and \texttt{repro\_amem\_links.py} reproduce the table's Mem0 and A-MEM rows from the frozen banks.
%% R112 (2026-09-15), review-2 A1: the Section 7 history-call sentence, moved here as the page swap the ruling named.
Mem0's per-memory history call, which keeps the prior text under a known identifier, was not consulted, so the overwrite reading is of the record as exposed and says nothing about the history log.

% R97-2b (SIDE registration S2; Amendment 1): the frozen census reading (registrations/ADDENDUM_SIDE.md section S2, 'prints regardless,
% appendix only'; ships verbatim, so the em dashes stand) and the S2-U quantities; NUMBERS_SIDE.md sections 5 and 8
% (AUX-SIDE-S2-CENSUS, -S2-CAP-RECALL, -S2-MINI); rounded forms in reviews/NUMBERS_R97.md. Section 6 carries the frozen branch text.
\paragraph{The store-written superseded list, and the renderer's cap.}
A registered follow-up asked whether a filter on the metadata FR-Bank itself exposes could recover the obligation gain the store-written summary loses.
Two candidate rules were fixed before any call, with a precision bar of $0.80$, and a census of the store decided it at no cost.
Two filters on the metadata FR-Bank exposes --- the store's own supersession pointers, alone and restricted to an active successor at least fourteen days later --- leave the superseded list at $15.04$ and $8.96$ names per item against $1.75$ under ground truth, at precision $0.035$ and $0.062$. The store records $5{,}968$ deactivations and the manifest $104$ supersessions, and on the fields the store exposes the two are indistinguishable. Forty of the $104$ are still active in the store. The believed-state loss is not a filtering problem.
The one condition that ran is descriptive: the recorded store-written store versions rendered with the per-category cap of fifteen names lifted.
The superseded-names section then runs to $32.55$ names per item against $15.04$ under the cap and $1.75$ under the ground-truth summary.
Its recall of the ground-truth names rises from $30.0\%$ to $66.6\%$ at precision $0.0348$ under the cap and $0.0357$ without it.
On the $661$ obligation questions the obligation AUROC reads blind $0.4946$, ground-truth $0.7339$, store-written $0.5959$, and store-written with the cap lifted $0.6513$.
The paired gains over blind are $+0.2393$ $[+0.1801,+0.2949]$, $+0.1012$ $[+0.0458,+0.1543]$ (persona-clustered $[+0.0217,+0.1744]$) and $+0.1567$ $[+0.0961,+0.2162]$ (persona-clustered $[+0.0552,+0.2536]$).
The lifted summary retains $65.5\%$ of the ground-truth gain, item-level $[44.6\%,87.2\%]$ and persona-clustered $[31.5\%,93.6\%]$, against the recorded $42.3\%$ under the cap.
The lifted-minus-capped difference is $+0.0554$, item-level $[+0.0110,+0.0992]$ and persona-clustered $[-0.0019,+0.1173]$, so the two intervals disagree and the wider one governs in Section~\ref{sec:backends}.
The three registered texts for this row (Appendix~\ref{app:registration}) read as follows.
%% SIDE S2 (registrations/ADDENDUM_SIDE.md section S2): the 42.3 percent sentence, ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 under
%% registrations/AMENDMENT_MAIN_PLACEMENT.md (R105, 2026-09-14; pending the author's ratification).
Under the \believed{} index the NOK gain is $+0.1012$ ($95\%$ CI $[+0.0217,+0.1744]$, with questions grouped by user profile). That keeps $42.3\%$ of the \trueidx{} gain, short of the $60\%$ we had set as the threshold.
%% SIDE S2-U not-expected-branch text (ADDENDUM_SIDE.md section S2; AUX-SIDE-S2-MINI): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 (same amendment).
Lifting the cap on the believed list, which raises its recall of ground-truth superseded names from $30.0$ to $66.6$ percent at unchanged precision, retains $65.5$ percent of the \trueidx{} gain, at or above the $60$ percent threshold.
Part of the believed-state loss recorded at $42.3$ percent is an effect of the renderer's cap of fifteen names rather than of the store's metadata.
%% The main study's frozen verdict sentence on the BELIEVED obligation row (PREREG_E1; CLAIMS.md): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 (same amendment).
NOK degrades materially under believed metadata but remains positive.

%% R126 addendum A7 (2026-09-20): Section 6's 'Haiku fares better' paragraph moved here in the readable body's words (AUX-BH-SPLIT: 0.8658 / 0.8855 on 53; 0.6839 on 10).
Haiku 4.5 fares better where FR-Bank keeps the target's name: with the uncapped store-written summary it scores $0.87$ on the supersession obligations whose name survives, against $0.89$ under ground truth, and $0.68$ where the name is missing (Table~\ref{tab:bh-split}). This descriptive split resembles the target-name ablation, but the missing-name subgroup is small and its paired decline is not reliable.

\paragraph{Haiku 4.5 under the store's own metadata.}
%% R110: ADDENDUM_BELIEVED_H (frozen 257a090; believed_h/NUMBERS_BELIEVED_H.md AUX-BH-LOCK, -LOOKUP-LIFTED, -H45-15, -H45-LIFTED, -RETENTION-15, -RETENTION-LIFTED, -BRANCH, -SPLIT).
A descriptive registration scored Haiku 4.5 at five calls per question on the recorded store-written texts under both renderings, the capped ($15.04$ superseded names per item) and the lifted ($32.55$ names, at most $50$), against its recorded blind ($0.4150$) and ground-truth ($0.8210$) rows on the full obligation set, with the name lookup on the same lists ($0.5858$ capped, $0.6588$ lifted, persona interval $[0.5777,0.7319]$).
Haiku 4.5 reads $0.7069$ capped ($[0.6657,0.7504]$; $[0.6620,0.7541]$) and $0.7364$ lifted ($[0.6911,0.7858]$; $[0.6665,0.8024]$), $-0.1141$ and $-0.0847$ below its ground-truth row, both reliable, and $+0.1212$ ($[+0.0628,+0.1849]$; $[+0.0317,+0.2107]$) and $+0.0776$ ($[+0.0272,+0.1355]$; $[-0.0031,+0.1498]$) above the name lookup.
Its retention, the ratio of the paired resampled differences, is $0.7190$ capped ($[0.6214,0.8497]$; $[0.5935,0.8603]$) and $0.7916$ lifted ($[0.6838,0.9243]$; $[0.6342,0.9425]$), at or above the sixty-percent line under both renderings.
The sixty-percent line was a conventional threshold, fixed before the main study, with no recorded reason for its value.
GPT-4.1 mini's retention figures above divide by an indexed-minus-blind difference that Appendix~\ref{app:laddertable} does not report as a gain, because its blind score is one value on $604$ of the $661$ questions, so Table~\ref{tab:levels} reports retention for Haiku 4.5 only.
Split by whether the obligation's own scrubbed name survives in the store-written list (Table~\ref{tab:bh-split}), Haiku 4.5 reads the $53$ supersession obligations whose name survives under the lifted rendering at $0.8658$ against $0.8855$ under the ground-truth summary ($-0.0197$, $[-0.0690,+0.0303]$; $[-0.0842,+0.0424]$), and the $10$ whose name is gone at $0.6839$ against $0.9122$ ($-0.2283$, $[-0.4288,-0.0430]$; $[-0.6568,+0.0182]$); under the cap the halves are $24$ and $39$, at $0.7993$ and $0.6933$.

\paragraph{What the store is in each scored condition, and where the labels come from.}
%% R131 (2026-09-20): R125 Task 3 (AUX-CF2-R125-T3-FRBANK); the paragraph on what the store is in each condition.
On FR-Bank the store is its own persona bank, read through the store's own fields (\texttt{is\_active}, \texttt{is\_retracted}, \texttt{supersedes}) by the one renderer into the store-written summary, capped at fifteen names per category or with the cap lifted.
The \pn{} labels come from the manifest, checked by hand, and each obligation's forbidding records are entries of that same bank, so the forbidding content is stored by construction for all $104$ obligations.
What the label does not say is whether the alignment of manifest events to stored records marks one of those records, which is what the ground-truth summary's superseded-names list and the ground-truth tags are built from: it marks $82$ at the $0.80$ bar, and the $22$ it does not mark are all retraction- or expiry-derived.
So on FR-Bank the label is manifest-relative, the ground-truth summary is alignment-relative and the store-written summary is store-relative; the three coincide on the $63$ supersession obligations and diverge on the counted states.
Table~\ref{tab:levels-strata} re-scores the rows of Table~\ref{tab:levels} on each stratum: on the $82$ the alignment marks, the capped store-written summary still costs GPT-4.1 mini $-0.1274$, Haiku 4.5 $-0.1258$ and the lookup $-0.2027$ against the ground-truth summary, each with both intervals clear of zero, so the loss the body reports is not an artifact of the obligations the alignment misses; on the $22$ it does not mark, the ground-truth summary itself reads $0.5210$ for mini, $0.7074$ for Haiku 4.5 and $0.4272$ for the lookup, because its list is built from the same alignment.
On Graphiti the store is one graph per persona built by the backend's own extraction from the same conversations with every lifecycle field removed, so whether it retains the forbidding content is a question of extraction (Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc-graphiti}, Table~\ref{tab:graphiti-strata}).
On Mem0 and A-MEM no obligation is scored: their scored populations hold \present{} and \absent{} items only, so their evaluation is the coverage judgment and the census, and there is no \pn{} label to state relative to what they retain.

Name lookup, lifted & $-0.1285$ $[-0.1842,-0.0716]$; $[-0.1993,-0.0615]$$^{*}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\paragraph{A fourth backend, measured on three personas.}
A trial on an unmodified public release of a fourth backend, Graphiti \citep{zep}, a temporal knowledge-graph store built by another group, asks whether the self-description problem is confined to the three backends above.
On three personas and $137$ episodes it names at least one superseded attribute per persona, so its summary is not degenerate.
Against the persona manifests it recovers $2$ of $10$ supersession events at the $0.80$ overlap bar this study inherited, a recall of $0.20$.
At the $0.75$ and $0.50$ bands the same census recovers $5$ and $6$, and three of the ten events sit between $0.75$ and $0.80$.
The verdict therefore turns on a threshold rather than on a wide margin, and all three bands are reported.
What it does mark is mostly not a manifest event, because precision runs $0.00$ to $0.04$ across the three personas.
Retraction and category are inexpressible under its stock defaults, so the summary emits the marker for both.
Three properties of the store account for most of the misses.
The user is split across three entity nodes in every persona, so a later fact about the same slot does not meet the earlier one.
About a tenth of edges carry no validity time, and invalidation requires one on both sides, and some temporal bookkeeping is incoherent, with one edge invalidated before its own validity begins.
The failure mode is loss of content with over-marking in what survives, the same direction as FR-Bank's at a far smaller absolute scale.
Because it covers three personas it is a trial rather than a census, and no obligation judgment is scored on this backend.
%% R131 close, A3 follow-up (2026-09-20): the earlier ingestion attributed in the third person to the release, which vendors that copy (graphiti/ and graphiti_patches/ in the freeze 5b097fb; FINAL RULINGS 5).
Disclosure, because it bears on independence: the FR-Bank release \citep{fr} built an earlier version of its store on a copy of this backend carrying four documented numeric-preservation modifications and ingested these personas into it.
Re-running the unmodified release on the same personas and episodes changes edge counts by $2.4\%$ and leaves supersession recall identical at the frozen bar, so those modifications are near-inert on this corpus.
Since the trial, the census and both monitoring routes were run on all forty personas (Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc-graphiti}). The name lookup retains useful signal; native tags add no established monitoring gain.

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Own-name ablation, retrieved-view monitors, the rubric gate, and the downstream experiment}\label{app:resp}

Three mechanism tests and one downstream experiment act on the judgment.
Each was preregistered with its texts written before any call (Table~\ref{tab:registrations-b}), so every outcome below is read against a text that predates it.
Every monitor call is GPT-4.1 mini at temperature $0$.
The blind and ground-truth-summary rows are the main study's, read from the recorded rows.
Intervals are the item-level paired bootstrap with the interval grouped by persona beside it.
Where the two disagree the wider one decides, and the tolerance is $\pm0.05$.

%%V11:1994-2003%%

The draw-noise floor, the same $102$ prompts sent once more, moves the whole-population score by $+0.0179$ ($[-0.0117,+0.0523]$).
The ablation's move on the same items, $-0.3250$, therefore lies outside the noise on every state that has positives among them.
On retraction-derived items, which the summary only counts, the ablation moves GPT-4.1 mini's discrimination by $-0.1404$ with interval $[-0.2569,-0.0508]$, outside the tolerance.
The change cannot come from a name the positives carried, so it comes through the negatives whose names were removed, or through the model's response to a section that now reads ``(none)'' against non-zero counts.
Eleven of the $26$ retraction-derived obligations carry their own name in the section the ablation edits.

\paragraph{The tagged view under the store's own tags, and Haiku 4.5 on the retrieved-view monitors.}
A descriptive registration re-rendered the same five retrieved records with FR-Bank's own lifecycle tags in place of the ground-truth join: the store's inactive flag as superseded, its retracted flag as retracted, and an event time before the persona's cutoff as expired, the rule the store-written summary uses.
The store's own tags change the tag on $1{,}273$ of the $3{,}305$ lines shown, and against the ground-truth non-active flag the store's tag has precision $0.14$ and recall $0.87$ over lines and precision $0.67$ and recall $0.78$ over the forbidding records in view.
GPT-4.1 mini on that view scores $0.8144$ against $0.6851$ under ground-truth tags, a difference of $+0.1292$ with intervals $[+0.0666,+0.1826]$ and $[+0.0606,+0.2060]$, and $+0.0805$ against the ground-truth summary (Table~\ref{tab:resp-l2}); under the registration's reading rule the view does not collapse (the fall is not a reliable one beyond the tolerance), so the system-builder rule on tags stays unconditional.
The same registration ran Haiku 4.5 at five calls per question on the hybrid monitor, then the tagged view, then the own-name ablation, in that order; hybrid ran, retrieved ran, the own-name ablation ran (Appendix~\ref{app:economics}).
Against its own summary row Haiku 4.5 gains $-0.0347$ shown both and $-0.1077$ on the tagged view alone, so the two routes read for the second model as they do for the first.

Without the detection step of the two-step rubric, GPT-4.1 mini's blind obligation score takes $0.0$ on $604$ and $0.1$ on $57$ of the $661$ questions, two distinct values.
Its one-sample discrimination is $0.4888$ with interval $[0.4646,0.5165]$, so the constant was not the gate.

\paragraph{Retrieval that returns tags, as configured.}
The retriever is the store's own cosine search over every entry the item's store variant holds, active or not, on the bank's cached embedding vectors.
It applies no lifecycle mask, no lexical channel, no category forcing, no multi-hop and no distillation, so every entry can be returned; the top ten are taken and the top five used.
Each returned line carries the lifecycle flags the store's ground-truth join records, the category, the scrubbed attribute, the age in days, and the text.
That is a modification of FR-Bank's stock configuration, which masks non-active records, and it is what makes the tags a route at all.

The store's ground-truth join flags the forbidding record as non-active for $82$ of the $104$ obligations.
The other $22$ retrieve as active, so no fetch by lifecycle state reaches them and the oracle's ceiling is $0.7885$ by construction; its reach of $99$ counts the $17$ of those $22$ that the retriever returns anyway.
With the forbidding record in view and its tag shown, GPT-4.1 mini gives the stale value as the answer in $11$ of $82$ cases at the gold threshold, and in $6$ of $17$ where the record appeared as active.
The retention of the oracle's gain, $0.7561$, is a near miss under the stricter interval convention, with $[0.563,0.939]$ grouped by persona against $0.60$.
The registered text (supplement, Section~S.3) reports the same interval at four decimals.
The $22$ unflagged records split by the fault in FR-Bank's own write path.
For $17$ of them ($14$ retraction-derived, $3$ expiry-derived) the store's own metadata marks at least one forbidding record superseded and none retracted, so the retraction or expiry was recorded through the supersession mechanism.
For the other $5$ ($4$ retraction-derived, $1$ expiry-derived, four of the ten distinct questions) no forbidding record carries any lifecycle mark at all; this census was run after the results were known.
Two further faults of the write path came out of a forensic audit of the answer stage, run after the results were known and gated against every recorded per-row score (the downstream audit report in the artifact and its ledger, section 9).
Active duplicates: for $35$ of the $89$ supersession and retraction obligations ($21$ and $14$) the view holds an active entry whose text asserts the stale value, a second copy of the forbidding content that the ground-truth join tied to no lifecycle event, while the item's own forbidding record may sit tagged beside it.
Every stale answer of GPT-4.1 mini under the oracle ($16$ of $16$ on those $89$) and $14$ of Haiku 4.5's $19$ fall on such a view, and none of mini's falls where the stale content sits only on tagged lines.
Negation: the ground-truth join assigns the retracted state by token coverage of the original fact at $0.80$ and reads no negation, so for one question, in both store variants, the retraction statement itself (``decided not to launch \ldots\ Oud and Geometry'') carries the retracted tag while the plan records stay active, because ``launching'' is not ``launch''.
An answerer that obeys the tag then reads the withdrawal as withdrawn.
The tagged and untagged split of Table~\ref{tab:ds} counts the item's own forbidding records and does not see these duplicates.

%% R129 A2 (2026-09-20): the inverted tag corrected on paper (reviews/v22/results/r129_b9.json; AUX-R129-B9); nothing re-answered.
The negation case is one of the $26$ retraction obligations, the full-store view of that question, carrying an inverted tag because the ground-truth join reads no negation; its slot-ablated view carries the same tag but holds no line asserting the stale value, so it is outside the $35$ either way. Correcting the tag, the retraction statement active and the plan records retracted, moves the $35$ of $89$ to $34$ and the retraction count from $14$ to $13$. The oracle's stale answers split by whether the view holds an active line asserting the stale value $16$ against $0$ for GPT-4.1 mini and $14$ against $5$ for Haiku 4.5 as joined, and $15$ against $1$ and $13$ against $6$ with the tag corrected, both answerers having answered that question from the plan lines the view showed as active. Every answer-stage table was scored with the tag as joined and says so in its caption; nothing was re-answered.


The fourth downstream prediction could not be met by its own gate, because the gate's score was elicited under the summary that only counts the retracted and expired states.
A descriptive repair registered afterwards elicited GPT-4.1 mini's obligation score once more under the summary that names all three states, on the same $661$ questions and under the main study's protocol, and drove the all-states fetch from it.
That score reaches an AUROC of $0.7354$ against the recorded $0.7339$, a paired difference of $+0.0015$ with intervals $[-0.0385,+0.0494]$ and $[-0.0473,+0.0503]$.
Naming the counted states in the summary therefore does not change GPT-4.1 mini's whole-population judgment; per state it reads $0.8790$ on supersession, $0.5941$ on retraction and $0.3770$ on expiry.
Thresholded at the same base rate the new gate fires on $110$ items ($63$ obligations, $22$ present, $25$ absent) against $75$ for the recorded gate, and the two agree on $65$.
The policy that reads it puts the forbidding record in view for $89$ of $104$ obligations ($0.8558$; $54$ of $63$ supersession, $22$ of $26$ retraction, $13$ of $15$ expiry; $72$ tagged).
It abstains as the model rule does, and it keeps coverage at $60$ of $174$.
On the $41$ counted-state obligations it reaches $0.8537$ against the recorded gate's $0.7805$, a difference of $+0.0732$ whose item-level interval $[+0.0000,+0.1707]$ and persona-clustered interval $[+0.0000,+0.1875]$ both reach zero.
The reason is that the new gate fires on only $5$ of the $17$ records naming makes fetchable, $3$ of them records the retriever misses.
In the answer stage its $78$ answered obligations give the stale value in $0$ cases at the high-precision threshold and in $10$ of $67$ tagged and $2$ of $11$ untagged cases at the gold threshold.

A second descriptive registration ran the answer stage for the literal-threshold lookup rule, which fetches whenever the name lookup's cosine is at or above the base rate and never abstains, under the protocol above.
Of its $268$ answered views, $238$ are byte-identical to a view a registered policy had already assembled and reuse the recorded answer, and $30$ were new calls.
Its $94$ reached obligations give the stale value in $0$ cases at the high-precision threshold and in $7$ of $75$ tagged and $7$ of $19$ untagged cases at the gold threshold, and its $174$ licensed items are answered correctly in $46$ cases, as under retrieval alone (Table~\ref{tab:ds}).
The same registration ran Haiku 4.5 as a second answerer on every distinct view of the seven policies, $452$ views, at temperature $0$ with the same two prompt texts (Table~\ref{tab:ds-h45}).
Haiku 4.5 writes longer answers, so the high-precision threshold no longer reads zero, and at the gold threshold it gives the stale value about twice as often as GPT-4.1 mini under every policy, with the untagged records again the worse half.
A third descriptive registration answered the same $452$ views once more with one instruction added to the prompt: to treat every entry tagged superseded, retracted or expired as not to be used, and to report the store as not holding the answer when only such entries bear on the question.
Under it GPT-4.1 mini gives the stale value at the gold threshold in $10$ of $54$ cases under retrieval alone and $18$ of $99$ under the oracle, against $9$ and $17$ without it, and Haiku 4.5 in $17$ and $24$ against $19$ and $27$.
Haiku 4.5's stale answers at the high-precision threshold fall from $7$ and $9$ to $3$ and $3$, and correct answers move by at most one under either answerer (the lower rows of Tables~\ref{tab:ds} and~\ref{tab:ds-h45}).
The instruction addresses tagged lines, and the audit finds the stale sources untagged: GPT-4.1 mini's answers are byte-identical under it on $310$ of the $452$ views.
A fourth amendment, registered on day $35$ of the registration record with two predictions, adds to the user message a header naming the persona and pinning the current date, because the view prints every entry as ``User'' and gives ages without a date.
The persona's name appears in $47$ of the $174$ licensed-item views while $172$ of the $174$ questions name the persona; $15$ of GPT-4.1 mini's $28$ in-view misses under retrieval alone are abstentions with the record on the page, and Haiku 4.5 gives that mismatch as its reason in $94$ of its $145$ abstentions on those items.
It predicted, before any call, that the correct count on the $65$ licensed items in view would rise reliably by at least $0.10$ for each answerer, and that the in-view abstentions would fall by more than half: from $15$ to $7$ or fewer for GPT-4.1 mini, from $38$ to $19$ or fewer for Haiku 4.5.
Both predictions cleared their bars for both answerers.
GPT-4.1 mini's correct count on the $65$ rose from $37$ to $48$, $+0.1692$ with intervals $[+0.0769,+0.2769]$ at item level and $[+0.0678,+0.2603]$ grouped by persona; Haiku 4.5's rose from $38$ to $59$, $+0.3231$ with $[+0.2000,+0.4462]$ and $[+0.1833,+0.4714]$.
In-view abstentions fell to $3$ for each answerer, and the abstentions the detector had counted correct fell from $12$ to $3$ for Haiku 4.5.
Over the $174$ licensed items the correct count rose from $46$ to $56$ and from $48$ to $73$; over the $221$ licensed-item views abstentions fell from $66$ to $42$ and from $145$ to $56$.
On the scorable obligations the header changed little for GPT-4.1 mini: $17$ of $85$ stale under the oracle against $16$ before, all $17$ on views whose active lines assert the stale value and none on the $50$ tagged-only or empty views.
Haiku 4.5, abstaining on $25$ of the $85$ against $44$ before, gave the stale value in $23$ of $85$ by the recorded detector, $15$ on the first sentence and $7$ from tagged-only views.
The expiry obligations, unscorable, show the pinned date at work: under the oracle GPT-4.1 mini abstains on $10$ of the $14$ and Haiku 4.5 on $13$, and Haiku 4.5's answers now say the arrangement's date has passed as of today's date.
Stale use prints by first sentence and by source beside the recorded column in the ledger (\texttt{AUX-DS-A4-*}).

% AUX-DS-AUDIT-RANK, AUX-DS-AUDIT-IDENTITY, AUX-DS-A4-STAGE2-* (moved whole from Section 9 under the R117 landing map; the pointer sentence "Two further results are in Appendix E.10" dropped as self-referential here)
A composite over all three jobs rises under the model rule by a reliable $+0.03$ for GPT-4.1 mini and stays level for Haiku 4.5 (Table~\ref{tab:ds-a7}).

Under the header prompt the composite under the model rule minus under retrieval alone is $+19$ of $661$ for GPT-4.1 mini, a rate difference of $+0.03$ with intervals $[+0.01,+0.05]$ at item level and $[+0.01,+0.05]$ grouped by persona, and $+3$ for Haiku~4.5 ($+0.00$, $[-0.01,+0.02]$ and $[-0.01,+0.02]$); the lookup rule reads $+25$ ($+0.04$, reliable) and $+9$ ($+0.01$, both intervals including zero).
The rise, where there is one, comes from the absent items the coverage rule abstains on and the answerer would have answered: on the five-line view the answerer already abstains on $241$ of the $383$ absent items (GPT-4.1 mini) and $280$ (Haiku~4.5), the rule adds $24$ and $12$ to those, and the licensed component falls by $3$ and $9$ where the rule abstains on a licensed item.
In the rule-only reading the same difference is $+167$ and $+163$ ($+0.25$, reliable for both answerers), carried entirely by the rule's abstentions, because retrieval alone has no coverage rule and counts none of the answerer's own.
Reported answers are rare for GPT-4.1 mini ($2$ of $74$ reached under the model rule, one of them by the successor alone) and a fifth to a quarter of the reached obligations for Haiku~4.5 ($16$ of $74$ under the header, $22$ with the duplicates tagged), most of them inside an abstention.
Agreement with the second annotator's fourth question is not reported: the validation is registered, with its workbooks built and no label collected at submission.

\paragraph{Answer-level outcomes under the policies (exploratory).}

%% R128 B7 (2026-09-20): both denominators (AUX-R128-DS-DENOM).
The stale and correct counts are a token detector's, read by one of us where flagged, with its limits in Appendix~\ref{app:resp}; its human validation is registered, with its workbooks built and no label collected at submission. The stale rate is printed over two denominators, every reached obligation the detector can score (supersession and retraction in view; expiry is unscorable) and the subset the policy answered, because the gated policies abstain on some reached obligations (the model rule answers $62$ of its $73$ reached scorable obligations, the lookup rule $58$; the top-five, literal and oracle policies abstain on none). The preregistered reading is stale use at the detector's $0.80$ threshold over the obligations whose forbidding record is in view and tagged, and it is zero under every policy. The counts and rates here are the descriptive reading at $0.50$; the Wilson intervals describe each policy alone and are not a paired test. The items on which each rule fires, the reach as a rate, the mean view size, tagged records and coverage are in Table~\ref{tab:ds-body-lower}.
\end{minipage}
\end{table}
Gating a lifecycle fetch on the obligation judgment reaches far more forbidding records than retrieval alone at the same budget.
On the full obligation set the reach rises from $0.52$ to $0.82$ of the obligations at a mean view of about seven records, where a seven-record retrieval view reaches $0.60$.
The lookup rule reaches the same $0.82$ and the oracle $0.95$ (Table~\ref{tab:ds-body}).
The lookup rule also costs correct answers on the licensed items, $40$ against $46$ under retrieval alone.
Stale answers do not fall under the gated fetch, because the store keeps an untagged active duplicate of the superseded content.
Tags in view are respected: the detector flags GPT-4.1 mini on $0$ of the $50$ views where every stale line is tagged or absent.
Each of its $16$ flagged answers under the oracle falls on a view with an untagged duplicate.
As a rate over the reached scorable obligations, the detector flags GPT-4.1 mini in $0.19$ of cases under retrieval alone and $0.19$ under the oracle, and the gated rules read $0.13$ and $0.10$ (Table~\ref{tab:ds-body}).
On the $42$ obligations retrieval alone reaches, the oracle's flagged count is $9$ against $8$, two answers turning stale and one turning clean, so no paired comparison separates the policies.
Retagging the store's untagged duplicates, and nothing else, removes most of the detector-flagged stale answers, which points to the write path.
The retagging is reference-assisted, the lines identified with the obligation's reference information and the detector, so it is a diagnostic intervention rather than a deployable repair.
Retagging the $35$ duplicates the audit found cuts flagged stale answers under the gated fetch from $0.15$ to $0.03$ for GPT-4.1 mini, over all reached obligations, a wider denominator than Table~\ref{tab:ds-body}'s scorable set.
For Haiku~4.5 the rate falls from $0.37$ to $0.23$; the licensed views were unchanged by construction, so this measures no cost on them.
Haiku~4.5's residual comes from tagged lines, mostly a mention of the tagged content beyond the answer's first sentence (Table~\ref{tab:ds-a5}).

% R109: the four verdict sentences moved verbatim from Section 8 (page-9 gate); the coverage rule's appendix pointer dropped as a self-reference.
The coverage rule at the base rate costs $-0.03$ of coverage on the $174$ items, a small loss with both intervals excluding zero.
The two registered bars were a reach gain of $0.50$ for the gold-label fetch and $60\%$ retention of that gain for the gated fetch.
The two bars that did not hold, the reach gain of $0.50$ and the $60\%$ retention, were set under the masked-retrieval premise (the $5\%$ pass rate), which the reach of $0.52$ replaced.
The gated fetch retains $0.76$ of that gain, and under the weaker reading, the interval grouped by persona, the lower end of $0.56$ falls below the $60\%$ bar.

\paragraph{The registered paragraph.} The downstream experiment's registered paragraph reads, with its numbers, as follows.
%% DS frozen Section 7 paragraph (registrations/ADDENDUM_DS.md section PAPER, frozen a5a2036, R100; AUX-DS-PARAGRAPH-PRINTED): R78-2c, frozen wording ships
%% verbatim; moved here from Section 8 under registrations/AMENDMENT_MAIN_PLACEMENT.md (R105, 2026-09-14; pending the author's ratification).
Acting on the obligation judgment changes what a bounded view carries. The store's truthful metadata flags $82$ of the $104$ forbidding records; the other $22$ ($18$ retraction-derived, $4$ expiry-derived) are a store-side fact of the kind Table~\ref{tab:believed} records, retrieve as active, and are out of reach of any policy that acts on the metadata. On the $104$ obligation items, the five-entry similarity view contains the forbidding record for $0.5192$; a gold-label fetch of the store's lifecycle records for the obligation's own attribute reaches $0.9519$, $0.4327$ above retrieval alone and short of the $0.50$ bar; gated on the smallest model's committed obligation score at its base rate, the fetch reaches $0.8173$, retaining $0.7561$ of that gain on the supersession-derived items (interval $[0.610, 0.872]$; $[0.562, 0.939]$ with questions grouped by user profile), short of the $60\%$ bar under the weaker reading. The superseded-names lookup reaches $0.8173$ at the same fetch budget, within tolerance of the model. Naming the retracted and expired attributes raises the gated reach on those obligations only from $0.7805$ to $0.7805$: the gate elicited under the counting summary does not fire on them. The coverage gate at its base rate costs $0.0287$ of coverage on the items whose licensed record the store holds, within tolerance. With the forbidding record in view and tagged, the smallest model gives the stale value as the answer in $0$ of $82$ cases at the registered detector threshold ($11$ of $82$ at the join's gold threshold; $6$ of $17$ where the record printed as active) (Appendix~\ref{app:resp}).

\paragraph{Reach by ranking depth, and the rates over the whole populations.}
The recorded ranking is ten deep, so the reach of a $k$-item retrieval view is a census over the $104$ obligations for $k$ up to $10$ (Table~\ref{tab:reach-k}).
The gated policies' mean view on those items is $7.08$ records for the model rule and $6.88$ for the lookup rule, so the seven-item view is their matched comparison.
The all-states policies at $10.74$ and $11.42$ records are matched by the ten-item view, the deepest recorded.
Stale use and correctness in Table~\ref{tab:ds-body} are counted over all $104$ obligations and all $174$ licensed items, so that an abstention or an unreached record counts as neither a stale answer nor a correct one.
Table~\ref{tab:ds} counts them over the answered subset.
The registered detector threshold of $0.80$ was calibrated on entry texts.
On a two-sentence answer the stale value's unique tokens are paraphrased, with coverage $0.5$ to $0.67$ on the answers that give the stale value.
That row therefore reads zero under every policy, and the gold threshold of $0.50$ is the sensitive one.
The audit discloses ten limits of the instrument, in one place.
First, the $15$ expiry obligations are unscorable for stale use, because the reference the detector holds for them is the expired fact itself, so the recorded column counts a mention of an expired arrangement as its use; the audit's own columns run on the $89$ supersession and retraction obligations.
Second, five retraction obligations cannot be flagged even when the store's own line is echoed verbatim; third, $6$ successor entry texts would be flagged as stale if echoed; fourth, $3$ licensed items cannot be marked correct even when their gold line is echoed.
Fifth, the flag is one-directional token coverage and rewards length, with an odds ratio of $2.34$ for GPT-4.1 mini and $2.99$ for Haiku 4.5 per doubling of answer length; on the first sentence alone Haiku 4.5's oracle count is $8$ of $85$ against $19$, and cut to GPT-4.1 mini's length it is $7$.
Sixth, for $35$ of the $89$ scorable obligations the view carries an active line asserting the stale value; seventh, one retraction statement carries the retracted tag because the ground-truth join reads no negation; eighth, the persona's name is bound to ``User'' in none of the views.
Ninth, the licensed record's median best rank under the retriever is $8.5$; tenth, twelve of Haiku 4.5's $38$ in-view abstentions on the $65$ licensed items were counted correct because their explanation restates the gold line.
As rates on the reached scorable obligations, retrieval alone and the oracle read $0.19$ and $0.19$ for GPT-4.1 mini and $0.26$ and $0.22$ for Haiku 4.5; on the $42$ obligations both policies reach, GPT-4.1 mini's stale answers move from $8$ to $9$ and Haiku 4.5's stay at $11$, and the $43$ obligations only the oracle reaches add $7$ and $8$.
Read by one of us, GPT-4.1 mini gives the stale value in $11$ of its $16$ scorable oracle flags and Haiku 4.5 in $8$ of its $19$, the rest naming the value while stating it superseded or restating the question's words; those classes are one reader's and not an independent validation.
A validation sample of $100$ answers is stratified across retrieval alone, the gated policies and the oracle, and across tagged and untagged reach.
Two annotators label whether the answer gives the stale value, whether it surfaces it, whether a licensed answer is correct, and whether the answer correctly reports the obligation; their agreement with each other, and with the detector at both thresholds on the first three, is not reported: the validation is registered, with its workbooks built and no label collected at submission.
The second annotator's sheet also carries the $19$ answers the audit read as giving the stale value ($11$ GPT-4.1 mini, $8$ Haiku 4.5), so the decisive labels receive two human reads.

\paragraph{What the gate buys, reach only.}
%% R123: R122 T5 (reviews/r122/registrations/ADDENDUM_R122_GATE_REACH.md, 77bc0d9; reviews/r122/NUMBERS_R122.md AUX-R122-GATE-*; reviews/v21/results/reach_rows_r123.json for the recorded policies' 661-means).
Three policies with no monitor call and no answer call, assembled from the recorded ranking and the store's records, are read against the model rule at its mean view of $5.43$ lines over the $661$ questions (Table~\ref{tab:gatereach}); over the $104$ obligations alone the same view averages $7.08$ lines, the denominator of Table~\ref{tab:ds-body-lower}.
A fetch keyed on the name the lookup matches best, with no gate, reaches $0.8164$ at the matched budget against the model rule's $0.8173$, a difference of $-0.0009$ with both intervals including zero.
Unmatched, it spends $6.51$ lines per question against $5.43$, a ratio of $1.20$, and cut to the matched budget it loses licensed records reliably ($-0.0375$).
The recorded similarity view at seven lines reaches $0.5962$, and matched $0.5443$.
A view that keeps only the newest record of each attribute reaches $0.2337$ matched, $-0.5836$ below the rule, a reliable difference, and none of the $26$ retraction obligations, because a cancellation is never the newest record of what it cancels.
All three predictions came out as written.

All three predictions came out as written: the ungated fetch within $0.05$ of the model rule at the matched budget, its unmatched form spending more lines, and the recency-aware view below the rule by more than $0.05$.}

\subsection{Spend and caching}\label{app:economics}

Monitoring runs before every query, so its cost turns on prompt caching.
The projection and the billed micro-run are in the supplement (Section~S.8).
%% R110: A5_EDITS.md item 6; one line per run from its ledger key (AUX-RESP-SPEND, AUX-DS-SPEND, AUX-RESP-A1-SPEND, AUX-DS-A1-SPEND to -A7-SPEND, AUX-CF2-SPEND, AUX-CF3-SPEND, AUX-PAD-SPEND, AUX-PARA-DRAFT, AUX-BH-SPEND, AUX-V2RCAT-SPEND).
Every model run in this paper was capped before its first call and metered from its rows at list or batch prices; the amounts, in dollars, are: the mechanism tests $0.94$ and the downstream experiment $0.11$; Haiku 4.5 on the retrieved-view monitors $6.72$ of a $12$ cap; the downstream answer-stage registrations $0.46$, $0.41$, $0.56$, $0.54$ (of $8$), $0.10$ (of $2$), $3.63$ (of $6$) and $0$; the counterfactual set $1.08$ of $22$ (OpenAI) and $72.50$ of $130$ (Anthropic); its expiry extension $2.21$ of $5$; the list-length check and its amendment $7.89$ of $28$; the paraphrase drafting run $0.12$ over $413$ calls; the long-list check on the counterfactual set $8.11$ of $12$ (Anthropic) and $15.54$ of $20$ (OpenAI); Haiku 4.5 under the store's own metadata $9.36$ of $12$; the increment moved within the triple $3.23$ of $4$; the forty-persona Graphiti ingestion $11.88$ of a $13.22$ stop, twelve aborted partial runs included (Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc-graphiti}); two later runs, the composite's answers on the actual expanded views \$$0.14$ and Opus 5 on the moved-count control \$$4.52$, under a \$$40$ ceiling for that pass.
%% R116 (2026-09-19): the price constants behind the two frontier lanes of the expansion (r74/tools/x4_run.py LANES; r74/NUMBERS_R74.md X4) and OpenAI's model pages of 18 September 2026 (reviews/NUMBERS_R116.md AUX-R116-PRICES).
GPT-5.6 Luna and GPT-5.6 Sol on the full populations were metered at $0.20$ and $1.20$, and at $5$ and $30$, dollars per million input and output tokens, the price constants of the full-population run code, to $2.67$ and $12.39$, the Sol run stopped; OpenAI's model pages of 18 September 2026 list $0.20$ and $1.20$ for Luna and $4$ and $20$ for Sol, with the first send of a long prompt billed at $1.25$ times the input rate, so the recorded figures are metered estimates rather than invoices.
The Graphiti follow-up's cumulative token-accounted list cost is $22.025336$ under that same ceiling; conservative input allowances and retained failed-request reservations put the upper accounting total at $26.825194$.
This includes completed Graphiti monitoring and answer generation, the repair answers, and both automated judging passes; the cached-depth judging that remains was not run.

\section{Registration record}\label{app:registration}

This appendix is the registration record: each frozen artifact, its commit or file hash, its freeze day, scope and outcome.

The September additions were registered before their runs but after earlier results on the same items were known; we claim no confirmatory design for them.

\noindent\textbf{How each freeze is attested.} Each runner wrote a lock record at its start holding the registration's sha256, and dated additions sit below the registered-text marker, outside the hash. For most registrations a commit holding the registered text also predates the run's first output. For CF-3, committed 33 minutes after its first call, and for DS Amendments 1 to 4, Q4, RESP Amendment 1 and Graphiti Amendment 2, first committed on day $39$, the evidence that the text predates the run is the runner's lock record and the file times.\\

\noindent\textbf{When Opus 5 entered.} Opus 5 entered the study at the cross-vendor preregistration (the cross-vendor addendum, Table~\ref{tab:registrations}; day $4$), carrying three of its predictions. That freeze came after the main-study freeze of day $0$ and before the counterfactual freeze of day $19$. In the counterfactual set it is a descriptive run carrying no prediction, because it was named at the freeze and funded before its run was submitted.\\

\noindent\textbf{Deviation index.} Every deviation named in this paper is indexed in the supplement (Section~S.2), with the ledger entry and the appendix that discusses it, because the index is a lookup aid for the artifact rather than a result.

\section{Analyses done after seeing the data}\label{app:posthoc}

This appendix collects analyses done after seeing the data, alongside the registered Graphiti follow-up in Section~\ref{app:posthoc-graphiti}.
The exploratory analyses carry no registered prediction and are not read as though they had been predicted.
The analyses marked ``specified before recomputation'' (the PN-versus-PRESENT decomposition, the intent-aware lookup, the whole-map coverage lookup and the reading of the forty-persona census) were written up as definitions with expected outcomes in one file, hashed before any of them ran (sha256 \texttt{ae2600fc}), and are post-hoc analyses, not predictions made before the original study.

\subsection{Calibration after the fact}\label{app:posthoc-cal}

The elicited score is rescaled by three monotone maps \citep{platt2000,zadrozny2002,guo2017} under leave-one-persona-out cross-validation on the pooled hard-tier coverage set.
That set holds $588$ questions.
No question therefore contributes to the fit that predicts it.
Expected calibration error on the intact-summary score falls from $0.1741$ to $0.0146$ under an isotonic map and $0.0029$ under a logistic one.
That comparison needs its floor, and the floor is a constant predictor.
At a base rate of $5.95\%$ a constant predictor scores an expected calibration error of $0.0000$ and a Brier score \citep{brier1950} of $0.0560$.
The calibration numbers alone therefore understate how little is being asked.
Against that floor the rescaled score improves the Brier score to $0.0519$.
It also improves selective risk \citep{geifman2017} from $0.0615$ to $0.0373$, which is where its resolution shows.
Figure~\ref{fig:reliability} is the reliability diagram behind these numbers.

%%V11:2161-2177%%

The rescaled probability never exceeds $0.375$ on the intact summary or $0.450$ on the names-only summary, so the value at which it would cross one half does not exist on either.
The comparison is therefore reported at each population's own base rate, where the crossings sit at $0.3$ and $0.2$ in score space.
At that threshold the rescaled intact-summary score flags $134$ of the $588$ hard questions and catches $24$ of the $35$ covered ones, a precision of $0.1791$ and a recall of $0.6857$ under the isotonic map.
Precision is low because the map's probabilities crowd the base rate, so the threshold that recovers most positives admits more than four false alarms for each true one.
Ordering is unchanged by construction, since every map is monotone.
Monotonicity is checked on each fitted map rather than on the pooled out-of-fold values.
Those pooled values are a mixture of forty maps, so their pooled ordering statistic moves.
On the question-only condition it reads $0.2572$ against a raw $0.4946$, a diagnostic of the pooling rather than a property of any map.

\subsection{A disclosure: the counterfactual set split by whether the question names its attribute}\label{app:posthoc-flags}

We read every question of the counterfactual set before scoring and flagged it when it names the plan or value its attribute is about (Appendix~\ref{app:counterfactual}).
The flag belongs to the question, so it is identical across the three store versions and cannot carry the label.
Table~\ref{tab:flag-split} splits the $444$ items by that flag, $89$ questions flagged and $59$ not, and reports every reader on each half.
The split answers whether the name lookup's score rides on questions that name what they ask about.
It does not: every reader scores higher on the questions that name only the topic, the name lookup at $0.8668$ against $0.7142$.
The split leaves the naming boundary in place on both halves.
It was made after the results were known, carries no prediction, and has no interval, since it is a deterministic census on the recorded rows.
It is a disclosure rather than a control, because the flag marks a question that names its attribute at the level of the topic while every value is scrubbed from every summary, so a flagged question need carry no more of the label than an unflagged one; the control is the paraphrase check reported in Appendix~\ref{app:counterfactual}, with this split recomputed on the rewrites (Table~\ref{tab:para-flags}).

%%V11:2194-2215%%

\subsection{The lookup's loss under a real backend's faults, decomposed}\label{app:posthoc-faults}

% R106 (2026-09-14): post hoc; nb6_sweep/NUMBERS_NB6SWEEP.md AUX-NB6SWEEP-DECOMP-444, -DECOMP-661, -BELIEVED, -PRINTED (from nb6_sweep/results/step4_recall.json).
A sweep run after every registered result was known asks how much of the name lookup's loss under FR-Bank's own list is due to each of three faults the census of Section~\ref{sec:backends} measured.
The faults are missing names (the list's recall of the ground-truth superseded names, $0.36$), wrong names (the share of negatives whose own name the list carries, $0.23$) and list length ($15$ names as rendered).
Each fault is injected into the ground-truth list at the store's measured rate, on the counterfactual set and on the $661$ questions, and the eight corners of the three-factor design give each factor's Shapley share of the total loss (Table~\ref{tab:faults}).
On the counterfactual set the embedding lookup falls from $0.77$ to $0.50$ under all three faults, and missing names carry about six tenths of that loss, wrong names about three tenths and length about one tenth.
On the $661$ questions it falls from $0.74$ to $0.61$; missing names carry about eight tenths and wrong names about four tenths, and length is a credit of about two tenths, because fillers drawn from the store's own names raise the absent probes less than the on-store items.
The three faults are sub-additive on both populations, since the single-factor losses sum past the total.
The sweep carries no prediction, and its report is in the artifact.

Post hoc; no prediction.}
\label{tab:faults}

%% R112 (2026-09-16): the forty-persona Graphiti census (registrations/ADDENDUM_R85_GRAPHITI_AMENDMENT_1.md frozen 43132a3; r85_graphiti/NUMBERS_R85.md AUX-R85-G10-INGEST, -CENSUS, -REPEAT, -CENSUS-FORBIDDING, -CENSUS-FORBIDDING-FULL, -PRINTED; DEVIATIONS_R85.md D-R85-12, -15, -16). No obligation judgment is scored on this backend.
\subsection{The fourth backend at forty personas}\label{app:posthoc-graphiti}

The three-persona trial of Appendix~\ref{app:census} was extended to every persona after the main results were known, under predictions written before the ingestion, and the reading of its population was fixed before any number here was printed.
The unmodified release (\texttt{graphiti-core} 0.28.1) ingested all forty personas at one episode per session: $1{,}365$ episodes rendered from FR-Bank's persona banks with every lifecycle field, status marker and supersession pointer removed, so the graph holds the same conversations FR-Bank holds and nothing of FR-Bank's own marks, and the ground truth is the persona manifest as everywhere in this paper.
The result is one graph per persona, $7{,}096$ entity nodes and $9{,}189$ edges; under the mapping of Appendix~\ref{app:census} (an invalidated edge with a successor is superseded, invalidated without one is expired, retraction inexpressible) $8{,}382$ edges are active, $130$ superseded and $677$ expired (the three sum to the $9{,}189$); inside those counts, $784$ carry no validity time and $72$ are dated after the persona's clock, an undated edge that no invalidation touches counting as active.
Every graph is the full store of its persona: the store variants of the main study, with categories or the successor removed, do not exist on it, so a Graphiti reading is valid only on the obligations of the full store, and the main text reports that population; the reading over all $104$ obligation rows, which repeats the same forbidding records on the same graph for the $57$ variant rows, prints below beside it because the prediction was written on it.

Precision is over all $807$ non-active edges of the forty graphs and is unchanged by the population. Retraction is inexpressible on this backend, so the eight obligations the ground truth marks retracted enter no recall.}
\label{tab:graphiti-g10}

By state on the full-store obligations, superseded preferences are recovered in $7$ of $16$, multi-version facts in $5$ of $11$ and expired arrangements in $1$ of $7$; over all $104$ rows the same three read $0.47$, $0.44$ and $0.09$, so expiry is the weak state on this backend under both readings.
The failure mode is extraction: of the $565$ manifest facts the graph asserts $134$ at the overlap bar, $77$ of the $109$ supersession events have no edge asserting the old value at all, and the user is split across two to four entity nodes in every persona, so a later fact about the same slot does not meet the earlier one.
The three trial personas, re-ingested into the forty-persona graphs, give the same recall on both graphs (elena $0.33$, tom $0.00$, yoli $0.25$) at $237$, $213$ and $224$ edges against the trial's $242$, $221$ and $222$.

The prediction fixed before the ingestion put the recall on the forbidding records at or below $0.25$ and the precision at or below $0.10$, the direction of the three-persona trial; the precision held and the recall did not, under the prediction's population and under both re-aggregations above.
%% The registered P1 outcome text as r85_graphiti/NUMBERS_R85.md AUX-R85-G10-CENSUS-FORBIDDING filled it; the registration's wording ships verbatim.
Its outcome text, quoted verbatim with the artifact's names (Appendix~\ref{app:glossary}), reads: ``Over the forty personas Graphiti reaches 30 of the 74 forbidding records (the join's count; 30 of the attack-vector 78), above the registered quarter, at precision 0.0669.''
What the census establishes is therefore narrower than the trial suggested: some of the records the agent must not use are recovered by this backend's own invalidation, most are not, and almost every non-active mark it writes falls on a record the ground truth calls active.
The registered monitoring routes have since run on the $323$ full-store questions, with $47$ obligations (Table~\ref{tab:graphiti-completed}).
The originally named $661$-item form was not produced: the full graphs contradict $230$ ablated-variant labels, so those variants cannot be treated as their own reduced stores.
This population correction is recorded before those calls; results do not silently inherit the larger denominator.
The lifecycle mapping uses invalidation and matching successor edges for supersession, maps invalidated edges without a successor to expiry, and leaves retraction inexpressible.
Names reduce to relation types under the stock node labels.

Amendment 1's three monitoring predictions (P2 to P4 in Appendix~\ref{app:registration}) are that the store-written summary falls below the ground-truth summary by more than the tolerance, that the superseded-names lookup sits within tolerance of chance, and that the tagged view sits within tolerance of the untagged view.
Its five readings are GR-1, that the paired obligation gain on the store-written summary excludes zero; GR-2, the same for coverage; GR-3, that the superseded-names lookup sits within tolerance of the model or above it; GR-4, descriptive retention against FR-Bank's ground-truth gain; and GR-5, the census's failure mode.
