The summary-route prediction holds; its registered text reads: ``Under Graphiti's store-written summary GPT-4.1 mini reads $0.5765$ against $0.7424$ under the ground-truth summary on the same questions, $-0.1659$ below with interval $[-0.2593,-0.0764]$; a matcher reading Graphiti's own list of superseded names reads $0.6780$.''
The lookup prediction fails: ``A matcher reading Graphiti's own list of superseded names reaches $0.6780$, $+0.1780$ from chance.''
The tag-route prediction holds: ``Shown the five retrieved edges with Graphiti's own states, GPT-4.1 mini reads $0.6854$ against $0.6995$ without them, within tolerance: the states this store writes do not reach the records that forbid use.''
GR-1's text reads: ``On a second store that records when a fact stopped being true, the obligation judgment does not rise beyond $+0.0801$ when the summary is supplied, so the gain measured on [the lifecycle store] does not reproduce where the lifecycle record is thinner.'' (The bracketed words stand in for the store's name as the registered text gives it.)
GR-3's reads: ``A matcher reading the store's own list of superseded names reaches $0.6780$ against the monitor's $0.5765$ on this store as well.''
The prediction that the native name lookup would remain near chance fails: its list carries useful matching signal despite weak summary monitoring.
For the capped summary minus question-only, the point difference is $+0.0801$; the item interval $[-0.0031,+0.1604]$ includes zero although the persona interval $[+0.0027,+0.1624]$ does not, so the weaker-interval rule makes GR-1 fail.
GR-2 is not computable: the full-store hard tier has $145$ questions and no coverage positives.
GR-4 is descriptive: native-summary gain retains $0.3257$ of the same-population ground-truth gain; GR-5 retains the extraction diagnosis.
The optional Haiku summary and hybrid readings remain unrun and their conditional rule is unevaluable.
The native tags' contrast does not establish equivalence, and detecting a correctly tagged obsolete edge is not discovering a backend defect.
The answer-stage comparison has generated its cached answers on $178$ common full-store items, plus $100$ FR-Bank-only ablated items, but its $622$-answer human sheet is blank.
Its registered human endpoints and prediction rulings were not completed at submission; the detector rows in the artifact are secondary and provisional.



\subsection{Exploratory source repair and practical-utility checks}\label{app:utility-completion}

These checks use repeatedly inspected development data and do not supply independent confirmation.
The original composite gains mainly reflect abstention on absent items; they do not establish correction of stale answers.
The existing repair comparison shares a coverage gate across five conditions: repaired top-five retrieval, an obligation-gated stale fetch, obligation-gated successor assembly, the same assembly without that gate, and matched-depth retrieval.
The complete population is $178$ items; all conditions retain $51$ coverage-gate exclusions.
The previously sampled $31$ questions span $24$ personas and include all five historical controls; the other $147$ questions remain semantically unjudged.
No answer or retrieval variant was regenerated.

We reproduced the original automated scores, then repaired every sampled reference packet by supplying all original user turns dated through the pinned query date, with session and turn identity retained.
The same reference rule applies to every condition, regardless of its answer or prior score.
Complete conversations remain available to human readers for context; assistant assertions are not personal-fact ground truth.
The original packets and judgments remain immutable.
The unchanged automated rubric was applied to $107$ distinct repaired inputs, representing $310$ answer cells.
For one question, a persona's hobbies, the original reference packet had omitted the session that supports the answer; with that source restored, the repaired judge marks every condition's answer correct for that question.
This is a new provisional judgment, not human adjudication or proof that every changed label was caused by the source addition alone.

\begin{table}[H]
\caption{Provisional automated outcomes on the fixed $31$-question repair sample. Strict correct / consequential stale endorsement; historical mention alone is not endorsement. All five conditions retain the common coverage gate. No registered human endpoint is replaced.}
\label{tab:repair-completion}
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
\toprule
Condition & GPT-4.1 mini & Haiku 4.5 \\
\midrule
Repaired top five & $9/5$ & $11/5$ \\
Obligation-gated stale fetch & $8/5$ & $10/3$ \\
Obligation-gated successor assembly & $9/5$ & $11/5$ \\
Successor assembly without obligation gate & $10/4$ & $12/4$ \\
Matched depth & $7/6$ & $11/5$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

Assembly without the obligation gate versus repaired top five gains one correct answer and removes one stale endorsement for each answerer, each an absolute $1/31$.
The correctness difference is $+3.23$ percentage points with persona-grouped interval $[0.00,+10.01]$; it does not establish a reliable practical improvement.
Against matched depth, the differences are $+9.68$ points $[0.00,+18.92]$ for GPT-4.1 mini and $+3.23$ points $[0.00,+10.01]$ for Haiku.
Adding the obligation gate loses the one gain for both answerers.
All condition contrasts, adverse transitions and uncertain-reference sensitivity are retained in the artifact.
Fourteen distinct judgments combine a determinate truth label with an insufficient or conflicting reference rating, and three quotations fail exact-substring checks; these defects are flagged, not silently relabeled.
The original $32$ blind audit inputs and seven supplementary inputs have repaired human packets, but no human labels.
Record-identifier reach, evidence delivery, gate decisions and answer behavior are separate measurements; none substitutes for semantic evidence of improvement.

The cached depth application fixes $557$ questions ($174$ licensed, $383$ absent), two answerers, and $139$ expansions from five to ten records.
Coverage-score selection, the cheap name lookup and random selection share that expansion budget; always-five and always-ten are reference policies.
Boundary ties and random selection are expectations, not observed integer counts.
The selectors, source rule, endpoints and analysis were frozen before new semantic labels.
Judging covered $203$ of $1{,}289$ distinct semantic inputs across eight personas before the configured provider reported exhausted credits.
The full population remains fixed; the other outcomes are missing, and full-population semantic point differences are withheld rather than imputed.
The completed cached mechanical comparison and the partial semantic results are exploratory, not a validated deployment operating point.
At this budget, recorded answer-generation charges are $0.136574$ and $0.449085$ under coverage selection, compared with $0.174832$ and $0.555921$ under always-ten.
The corresponding maximum selector allowances are only $0.038258$ and $0.106836$ per batch, before unmeasured retrieval, latency and maintenance costs.
Applying the historically measured mean monitor cost to this batch would add $0.289083$; that is a scenario based on recorded monitoring telemetry, not a new charge or a free-cache assumption.
Quality retention is unestablished, and local name encoding and summary maintenance have not been fully priced.
Practical utility therefore remains unresolved; the data support monitoring discrimination and specific failure mechanisms, not a claim of lower total cost or fewer human-validated stale errors.
The registered human evaluation and detector validation remain explicit dependencies.
